# Baseline category and historical-evidence inventory

Attachment to [the canonical evidence ledger](safejs-gap-closure-evidence.md). Captured source `d126d355c150a076e6c5d5f110162289c57ed1c9`; Node v22.23.2, ICU 78.2. No runtime code changed. Read root AGENTS.md, current SafeJS README, conformance discovery/metadata/execution/corpus source, language-completeness task definitions and the current gap checkpoint. Historical documents were inventoried by full content SHA-256; their claims are leads, never silently adopted as current results.

## Commit-scope inventory audit — 2026-09-12

This maintained attachment preserves the prior inventory's stable IDs and observations while removing dependencies on uncommitted raw artifacts. Relative links resolve to tracked repository documents; plain-code local historical paths identify user-owned evidence that is neither adopted nor required for category enumeration. Historical document contents can differ from their recorded working-copy fingerprints. The only original manifest mismatch observed before this audit was H-0424; its current hash is recorded in the canonical ledger. A preserved fingerprint records an observation, not a claim that the corresponding text shipped.

**Coverage:** 199 feature categories, 270 path categories (128 directories and 142 immediate JavaScript file paths), 955 historical records, 11 exploratory files and 40 explicit cases: **1,475 unique IDs**. The four additional timing cases below extract independent obligations from C-PERFORMANCE. Every row has one primary owner and inherits Gate G. IDs do not imply passing assertions.

**Acceptance scope:** every Test262 category and every historical evidence record is inventoried. Current known remaining gap families from both dated checkpoint inventories, README limitation/host-copy sections and all exploratory tests are reconciled to explicit cases or named feature owners below. The 955 historical documents have not undergone exhaustive claim-by-claim semantic qualification; that remains **unverified** under their H-row owners. No broad category completion can silently discharge a historical claim, and this baseline does not declare every past reported defect currently true or repaired.

Disposition vocabulary is orthogonal to execution: **defect** requires a reproduced deviation from the pinned normative/API contract; **unverified** includes unexecuted or ambiguous reports; **intentional boundary** identifies explicit host authority policy; **upstream limitation** records demonstrated backend constraints without assigning an ECMAScript defect; **verified** is bounded to its stated candidate/runtime/checks; **delivered** requires the exact fix on remote main; **published** additionally requires successful release and artifact receipts. Runner exclusions and unavailable runtime cells remain nonpasses.

## Finite inventory and interpretation

Pinned [Test262](https://github.com/tc39/test262/tree/419d3e0a2273ba01a3bfcbec423f2801425b8e93) clean checkout: `/private/tmp/safejs-baseline-test262-419d3e0`. Features source SHA-256 `2f8dfa3d3ba98ae4618692c30f7c365069fb55d5899add707f50749f648ba039`. Enumerated 53876 test-tree JavaScript files, 294 suffix-named fixtures, 199 distinct declared-or-observed feature names (199 declared), 18651 valid YAML headers without feature tags, 294 missing metadata headers (inventory exceptions; not runner errors). Feature counts overlap and include fixtures; they are not variant/pass totals. All files, including untagged files, are covered by the path inventory. Feature path-list digests and historical/exploratory source fingerprints are embedded in the tables below; the local historical manifest is optional audit material, not a committed dependency.

Upstream's “Standard language features” label is **not** an ES2025 membership assertion: this corpus is newer than the chosen published target. Until each fixture is reconciled to the pinned edition, disposition is unverified; newer Temporal, weak/upsert and Atomics APIs stay separately visible. ECMA-402, Annex B, harness helpers and staging are distinguished below. Staging is inventoried but excluded from normative target pass totals; required behavior also appearing there must be tested in its normative context. No unverified feature is an implicit pass or permanent exclusion.

## Gate G: required per unresolved row

Every row below inherits **all canonical runtime matrix cells** (minimum Node 18.18.0, CI Node 22, and each supported runtime cell defined by the canonical ledger). A runner missing on a runtime means unexecuted, not passed. Primary owner is the stable task ID printed in the row. These IDs are self-contained assignments; the local working language-completeness draft supplies their broader implementation prompts but is not a prerequisite to interpret this map.

- Specification/path and reproduction: use the exact pinned test path shown, then enumerate every fixture whose parsed YAML contains that feature. For directory rows enumerate all descendants; for immediate-file rows execute that exact file, including untagged fixtures, and preserve fixture/helper exclusions separately. For historical records, use the source document's reproducer and resolve its stated clause/path before accepting a defect. Include the smallest counterexample and a neighboring passing control. Native output is a control only.
- Regression: first reproduce at the current source/runtime; add a fast maintained failing test before any repair. An untracked test is exploratory evidence, never a shipped regression.
- Original/replay: preserve the upstream-declared original context (Script or Module), strict/non-strict variants, async completion, negative phase and error type, and declared agent/blocking requirements. Record unsupported modes without substituting a Script wrapper or dropping variants. Separately verify public SDK original, pending checkpoint and completed replay for affected observable behavior, or record why replay is inapplicable.
- Artifact probe: run the same observable counterexample/control through fresh installed package entrypoints on required runtime cells; source-only success cannot satisfy this gate.
- Release receipt: require exact source/fix SHA on remote main, workflow run with terminal publication success, registry version/integrity and installed-artifact probe. None of this inventory's unverified rows claims such a receipt. The canonical receipt table may prove package delivery without proving a category.

Historical rows are a record-level inventory, not a completed claim-by-claim census. Before closing a historical row, its primary owner must enumerate every still-actionable claim into stable child case IDs with individual dispositions and cross-feature dependencies, or link an existing case that covers it. Record which claims are reproduced, unverified, historical repairs, intentional boundaries or upstream limitations. A document title/hash alone cannot establish semantic closure; completion of one claim cannot close the others. The current explicit case rows below extract priority remaining leads from the September 10 checkpoint without claiming to have atomized all historical prose.

Shared dependencies for **every** category: `complete-conformance-runner` (enumeration/modes), `verify-conformance-oracles` (correct oracle), `qualify-realms-and-recovery` (original/replay), `verify-runtime-support-matrix` and `verify-package-provenance` (artifact), `verify-final-release` (receipt). Module categories additionally depend on `qualify-module-authority`; weak/Atomics on `qualify-lifecycle-and-retention`; Intl/Temporal on `repair-iso-month-formatting` and `repair-temporal-extremes`; symbol/Promise host copying on `repair-promise-symbol-admission`. The primary owner retains coverage when dependencies finish.

A feature row’s first path is a **sample metadata association**, not necessarily its best semantic counterexample: a fixture may tag several interacting features, and alphabetical order can select an Annex B or unrelated-family interaction. Primary owners must select the smallest relevant failing fixture and passing neighbor before any repair. Category IDs and path-list hashes remain stable; sample selection is not coverage.

## Every known historical SafeJS markdown record

955 records; each has a stable-at-baseline ID, one primary owner and Gate G. This registry intentionally retains records that claim a fix, old release holds, performance suspicions and overlapping audits. “Unverified” means their exact current-revision and runtime qualification is pending, not that every old fix is broken. Hashes in the manifest identify the exact working-copy evidence, including modified tracked files. Baseline evidence attachments are not recursively inventoried.

## Enumeration reproduction

At the exact clean pinned checkout, enumerate every `.js` recursively under `test/` in lexical path order; normalize CRLF/CR to LF in metadata only and parse each `/*--- … ---*/` block with the installed YAML parser, union `features` with noncomment entries in `features.txt`, count feature membership, preserve all parsing exceptions, and hash each sorted feature path list joined with LF plus a terminal LF. Count each top-level directory and immediate child path (including direct JavaScript file children) without discarding staging, fixtures, missing metadata or untagged files. The manifest contains exception paths and flags to independently reconcile this inventory. Historical record scope is every direct `docs/plans/safejs*.md`, excluding the canonical ledger being created; nested attachments are evidence, not additional historical cases.

## Edition boundary and precise Temporal extension contract

The published [ECMAScript 2025 text](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/) was downloaded and its element IDs inspected; its content SHA-256 is in `inventory-manifest.json` under `editionCheck`. Confirmed base examples are Float16Array (`sec-float16array`), RegExp.escape (`sec-regexp.escape`), Promise.try (`sec-promise.try`), Set methods (`sec-set.prototype.union`), Atomics.waitAsync (`sec-atomics.waitasync`) and eligible weak symbols (`sec-canbeheldweakly`). These categories still require implementation qualification; base membership is not a passing result.

Explicitly separate from that base edition: Array.fromAsync, explicit resource management, Temporal, Atomics.pause, Error.isError, Math.sumPrecise, Uint8Array base64, upsert, iterator chunking/inclusion/sequencing/joint iteration, and JSON parse-with-source. They remain visible extension categories, never silently added to ES2025. `features.txt` proposal-section rows remain proposal membership leads. All other unresolved membership rows retain their primary feature owner, with `drain-conformance-findings` responsible for checking completion rather than assuming membership from upstream's current “standard” heading. Intl402 uses its separately pinned ECMA-402 target, not ECMA-262 membership.

For C-TEMPORAL-EXTREME, the exact reproduction pin is Test262 `419d3e0a2273ba01a3bfcbec423f2801425b8e93`; it is a fixture pin, **not a version pin for the living proposal**. The primary extension specification is [Temporal §15.6.15 HandleDateTimeTemporalDate](https://tc39.es/proposal-temporal/#sec-temporal-handledatetimetemporaldate): it combines the ISO date with noon and carries epoch nanoseconds as a plain value. [§15.6.21 HandleDateTimeOthers](https://tc39.es/proposal-temporal/#sec-temporal-handledatetimeothers) separately applies TimeClip to numeric inputs. This distinction supports the valid minimum PlainDate counterexample and forbids “repairing” it by relaxing numeric-Date validation. The living proposal was checked during this baseline; fixture `esid: sec-partitiondatetimepattern` and its embedded algorithm excerpt are preserved by the pinned source hash. The immutable extension specification is now pinned to proposal-temporal revision `e8cc03fc970a65a3359e8870e3b35e687ac94e55`, verified directly in [spec/intl.html](https://github.com/tc39/proposal-temporal/blob/e8cc03fc970a65a3359e8870e3b35e687ac94e55/spec/intl.html#L1095). Its `HandleDateTimeTemporalDate` algorithm matches the no-TimeClip distinction; content SHA-256 is recorded in the manifest. `repair-temporal-extremes` must use this explicit extension pin and reconcile any proposed change to it; neither a newer living spec nor newer fixtures silently redefine acceptance.

## Independent commit-scope enumeration and documentation QA

Executed 2026-09-12 against clean Test262 `419d3e0a2273ba01a3bfcbec423f2801425b8e93`, source `d126d355c150a076e6c5d5f110162289c57ed1c9`, Node 22.23.2 / ICU 78.2. Read `features.txt` with inline comments removed; recursively read every test `.js`, normalize metadata line endings and parse YAML with installed `yaml`; accumulate feature paths using null-prototype maps; hash sorted paths joined by LF plus terminal LF (including the empty category's LF). Compare every feature count/digest and every top-level/immediate-child path count to the retained inventory. Terminal exit 0: 199 declared / 198 observed features; 53,876 JS files; 270 path categories comprising 128 directories and 142 immediate JS file paths; 294 missing headers, all retained fixtures; 18,651 untagged valid headers; zero YAML parse errors; zero count/hash mismatches. `export-defer` is declared with zero fixtures.

Two preliminary census attempts were QA setup errors, not product failures: an ordinary JavaScript object collided with the `__proto__` feature, then inline feature comments/empty-category newline and direct-child file treatment differed from the documented manifest. The corrected enumeration preserves those names and children explicitly; it does not delete categories to match counts. The old phrase “270 directory categories” is corrected here without altering any stable ID or path.

Manual review checked the pinned edition/proposal distinction, one primary owner per row, complete Gate G inheritance, current README/checkpoint/exploratory mapping, nonpass dispositions and source/release separation. Markdown structure, duplicate IDs, table cell counts and all required local links are checked before the evidence-only commit; untracked historical references are plain text and remain optional. No runtime code or tests changed, so no repair TDD or visual CLI screenshot is claimed.
